The term *al-ghuduww* refers to the early morning hours, the period of dawn when the world awakens, while *al-āṣāl* is the plural of *aṣīl*, denoting the late afternoon hours preceding sunset.

Together, these two temporal poles encompass the entire cycle of human wakefulness. Classical authorities noted that this glorification refers to the canonical prayers, specifically the dawn prayer (*Fajr*) and the afternoon and evening prayers (*ʿAṣr*, *Maghrib*, and *ʿIshāʾ*). By consecrating the beginning and the end of each day to the glorification (*tasbīḥ*) of the Lord, believers anchor their daily existence in cosmic praise. The sun rises and sets as the servant bows and prostrates, harmonizing human worship with the ceaseless hymns sung by the angels and the physical cosmos, ensuring that morning endeavors and evening rest are both sealed with divine remembrance and protected from heedlessness.

**Spiritual Resonance in Human Temples**

Beyond physical architecture, insightful spiritual masters observed a profound allegorical dimension in the term *buyūt*. The ultimate "house" built by God on earth is the human breast, and the true sanctuary wherein His Name is continuously remembered is the purified heart of the sincere believer.

Just as a physical mosque must be elevated above filth and consecrated for worship, the interior temple of the human heart must be cleansed of malice, arrogance, covetousness, and spiritual pollutants. When the heart is consecrated as a dwelling for the divine Name, the uncreated light of God shines through it without obstruction. The believer walks among fellow human beings as a living sanctuary of light, radiating integrity, compassion, justice, and tranquil majesty into every sphere of family and communal life, becoming an instrument of peace, healing, and guidance in society.

**Syntactic Continuity with the Verse of Light**

The structural placement of verse 36 immediately following verse 35 reveals a vital pedagogical truth: illumination requires dedicated discipline and sacred communal space. The magnificent metaphysical parabola of the Light of the heavens and earth does not conclude with solitary mystical withdrawal or abstract theological musing.

Instead, the divine discourse immediately guides the seeker toward the concrete communal reality of the mosque. If you seek to gaze upon the niche, the lamp, and the glowing crystal described in the Verse of Light, look into the houses of prayer where sincere souls gather to prostrate before their Maker. The highest spiritual ecstasy is anchored within the disciplined, communal routines of congregational worship, joining heaven's light to earth's obedience, and ensuring that spiritual illumination translates directly into social solidarity, communal warmth, and collective righteousness that shields the entire community from ethical collapse, establishing an enduring beacon of peace.

---
## Sūrah an-Nūr 24:37

> by men who are not distracted—either by buying or selling—from Allah’s remembrance, or performing prayer, or paying alms-tax. They fear a Day when hearts and eyes will tremble,

**Expanded Commentary**

**True Nobility and Divine Archetypes: rijālun lā tulhīhim**

The divine command identifies the noble inhabitants who populate the elevated houses of worship: *rijālun lā tulhīhim tijāratun wa-lā bayʿun ʿan dhikri Allāh*. The designation *rijāl* (men) in Qur'anic usage does not merely denote biological masculinity; it conveys moral fortitude, spiritual maturity, integrity, chivalry, and unyielding steadfastness in faith.

These are mature souls who have conquered the intoxicating distractions of material existence. They participate actively in worldly affairs, running businesses, managing trades, negotiating markets, and engaging in agriculture, yet their hearts remain unenslaved by commerce. When the call to prayer reverberates across the city, they immediately abandon ledgers, contracts, and clients, demonstrating that their highest loyalty belongs exclusively to the Creator. They are not ascetics who fled into monastic isolation; they are righteous actors whose worldly involvement never dims their inner spiritual vigilance, illustrating the perfect synthesis of active civic life and contemplative devotion across every arena of daily responsibility, proving that worldly involvement can coexist with profound holiness.

**Commercial Engagement Without Spiritual Compromise: tijāratun wa-lā bayʿ**

The revelation distinguishes between two forms of economic transaction: *tijārah* (general trade or business ventures) and *bayʿ* (immediate buying and selling contracts). Classical commentators noted the profound subtlety of mentioning both terms.

*Tijārah* represents large-scale, ongoing business enterprises that consume long-term cognitive focus, planning, and capital investment. *Bayʿ* denotes individual, quick retail transactions where immediate profit is exchanged hands-on. By declaring that neither long-term commercial ambition nor immediate cash profit distracts them, the text covers the full spectrum of economic temptation. These individuals operate within the commercial world without allowing the world to occupy their hearts. They handle silver and gold with their hands, but keep their souls entirely consecrated to the transcendent remembrance of God, remaining upright in both large contracts and petty exchanges, refusing to let material gain compromise ethical virtue, degrade personal integrity, or dim their inner spiritual vision, living as shining examples of righteousness.

**Tripartite Pillars: Dhikr, Ṣalāh, and Zakāh**

The verse delineates the three fundamental spiritual disciplines that safeguard the soul against material corruption: *ʿan dhikri Allāhi wa-iqāmi al-ṣalāti wa-ītāʾi al-zakāh*.

First, *dhikr Allāh*—the continuous remembrance of God—anchors the conscious mind in divine presence, preventing moral blindness during negotiations and shielding the conscience from deceit. Second, *iqām al-ṣalāh*—the timely establishment of canonical prayer—imposes sacred temporal pauses throughout the business day, breaking the relentless momentum of profit-seeking and reminding the merchant of ultimate servitude before the King of kings. Third, *ītāʾ al-zakāh*—the cheerful payment of obligatory alms—purifies accumulated capital from greed, hoarding, and stinginess, affirming that wealth is a divine loan meant to be shared with the vulnerable. Together, these three disciplines immunize the faithful from economic idol worship and social callousness, establishing a healthy balance between spiritual devotion and communal generosity, ensuring that wealth circulates justly.

**Eschatological Tremor: tataqallabu fīhi al-qulūbu wa-al-abṣār**

The inner engine that powers their steadfastness is a profound awe of divine accountability: *yakhāfūna yawman tataqallabu fīhi al-qulūbu wa-al-abṣār*, they fear a Day when hearts and eyes will tremble and turn about. The verb *tataqallab* depicts violent, erratic overturning, instability, and convulsions.

On the Day of Judgment, human hearts will palpitate uncontrollably between sheer terror and desperate hope for salvation, while eyes will dart restlessly, transfixed by overwhelming cosmic upheavals and celestial ruptures. The superficial illusions of worldly security, commercial wealth, and social prestige will evaporate instantly. Those who kept their eyes lowered and their hearts tranquil in devotion on earth will be sheltered on that Day, while those who allowed their eyes and hearts to wander in heedless pursuit of worldly gain will experience agonizing, unquenchable panic as reality is unveiled, realizing too late that their accumulated wealth cannot purchase deliverance from divine reckoning.

**Psychological Resilience in the Modern Marketplace**

This verse articulates an enduring philosophy of work and ethics that directly addresses contemporary economic pressures. Modern commercial life constantly demands total cognitive absorption, conditioning professionals to measure human worth purely through quarterly profits, career advancement, consumer prestige, and accumulated assets.

The Qur'an shatters this commercial absolutism, establishing that true human dignity is found in maintaining moral autonomy amidst economic competition. The believer does not allow career demands to devour spiritual duties, nor do they compromise honesty for financial advantage. By placing the remembrance of God at the pinnacle of their daily schedule, they achieve exceptional psychological equilibrium. They operate in high-pressure financial environments without succumbing to the anxiety, moral burnout, loneliness, and cynicism that routinely ravage purely materialistic societies, preserving their inner tranquility, ethical integrity, and moral clarity across every life trial.

**The Courage of Spiritual Detachment**

The spiritual profile drawn in verse 37 demands extraordinary moral courage. To walk away from a lucrative negotiation simply because the call to prayer has sounded requires an absolute conviction that sustenance (*rizq*) comes from God alone, not from aggressive commercial maneuvers.

The early companions exemplified this sublime detachment. Commentators recorded that when the blacksmiths among them raised their hammers to strike iron and heard the *adhān*, they would not even bring the hammer down upon the anvil; they would lay the tool aside immediately to hasten to prayer. Similarly, merchants weighing goods would leave their scales balanced and walk directly to the mosque. This unyielding prioritization of the eternal over the ephemeral transformed an obscure group of desert traders into the moral leaders of civilization, showing that worldly success reaches its peak when subordinated to sacred obedience, elevating commerce into an arena of spiritual integrity and selfless service, lighting up the marketplace with divine remembrance and ethical distinction.

---
## Sūrah an-Nūr 24:38

> ˹hoping˺ that Allah may reward them according to the best of their deeds, and increase them out of His grace. And Allah provides for whoever He wills without limit.

**Expanded Commentary**

**Divine Evaluation by the Best Standard: li-yajziyahumu Allāh**

The divine decree unveils the ultimate reward awaiting those noble souls who refused to let worldly commerce distract them from divine worship: *li-yajziyahumu Allāhu aḥsana mā ʿamilū*. The prepositional prefix *lām* in *li-yajziyahum* indicates both purpose and consequence; their disciplined devotion was oriented toward this singular divine reckoning.

The phrase *aḥsana mā ʿamilū* (the best of what they did) reveals an astonishing principle of divine justice and mercy. Classical commentators, including al-Rāzī, al-Ṭabarī, and al-Qurṭubī, explained that God does not evaluate the believer through a harsh, hyper-critical scrutiny that exposes every minor flaw and human imperfection. Instead, He takes the finest, most sincere, and most beautiful deeds performed by the servant and uses them as the standard measure by which the rest of their righteous conduct is judged and elevated. Minor shortcomings are graciously pardoned and overshadowed by the excellence of their highest devotions, filling the believer with profound hope, enduring gratitude, and unshakeable spiritual tranquility that sustains the heart across every trial, inspiring deeper love for God.

**Transcendent Grace Beyond Merit: wa-yazīdahum min faḍlih**

Divine reward is not confined to a strict one-to-one transactional equivalence: *wa-yazīdahum min faḍlih*, and increase them out of His grace. In the theological framework of the Qur'an, human salvation is never purchased merely by the mechanical weight of mortal deeds.

Even the most devout servant, having spent a lifetime in prayer, fasting, and charity, could never balance the scales against the infinite blessings of life, sight, intellect, and guidance bestowed by God. Therefore, while the *jazāʾ* (recompense) corresponds to the excellence of their deeds, the *ziyādah* (increase) flows entirely from unearned divine bounty (*faḍl*). God bestows unimaginable celestial honors, spiritual unveilings, and eternal joys that far surpass anything the servant earned or imagined, proving that paradise is an inheritance of divine grace rather than commercial wages, comforting the soul that knows its finite limitations and yearns for divine generosity to complete its salvation and grant it everlasting peace.

**Limitless Provision: Allāhu yarzuqu man yashāʾu bi-ghayri ḥisāb**

The verse concludes with an awe-inspiring cosmological declaration: *wa-Allāhu yarzuqu man yashāʾu bi-ghayri ḥisāb*, and Allah provides for whoever He wills without limit. The expression *bi-ghayri ḥisāb* (without reckoning) operates on multiple layers of divine generosity and transcendent majesty.

First, it means that God does not maintain a petty, restrictive ledger when showering His bounties upon His chosen servants; His divine treasury is infinite, incapable of diminution or exhaustion. Second, it signifies that the recipient does not have to reckon or calculate how sustenance will arrive; divine provision descends through completely unforeseen channels (*min ḥaythu lā yaḥtasib*), exceeding every material expectation and logical forecast. Third, in the Hereafter, the reward granted to the righteous is boundless in duration and intensity, devoid of any final balance sheet or termination, continuing in ever-increasing ecstasy throughout eternity without depletion, fatigue, or interruption, establishing everlasting bliss.

**Overcoming the Trap of Self-Righteous Calculation**

A profound psychological liberation occurs when the believer internalizes the truth of unreckoned divine provision. Materialistic culture conditions human beings to view life as an exhausting, zero-sum economic calculation where every effort must yield an immediate, measurable return.

This transactional mentality frequently poisons religious life, leading people into self-righteous arrogance if they perform rituals, or deep despair if worldly hardship strikes despite their prayers. Verse 38 frees the soul from this commercial neurosis. The believer realizes that their modest acts of worship are accepted and multiplied by a Lord whose generosity is boundless. They do not approach God as anxious accountants bargaining for wages; they serve Him with joyous, loving gratitude, trusting that His grace will reward them far beyond their humble efforts, sanctifying their service with sincere humility, deep love, profound reverence, and spiritual contentment that no worldly hardship can shatter.

**Cosmic Generosity and the Architecture of Divine Mercy**

Reflecting on the relationship between verse 37 and verse 38 illuminates the supreme coherence of Qur'anic pedagogy. In verse 37, the merchants willingly paused their commercial calculations—buying, selling, profit margins, and inventory—to honor God's remembrance.

In reciprocal divine justice, verse 38 assures these merchants that because they abandoned worldly calculations for the sake of God, God will provide for them without any calculation whatsoever! The finite profit they sacrificed in the physical marketplace is replaced by infinite spiritual and material sustenance descending from the Lord of the heavens and the earth. Giving up temporary, calculated wealth for divine pleasure unlocks the uncalculated, boundless abundance of the Creator, demonstrating the magnificent symmetry of divine mercy across both terrestrial and celestial existence, rewarding faithful restraint with eternal bounty, joy, honor, and spiritual elevation that endures forever.

**The Ultimate Vindication of Detached Devotion**

The promise embedded in this verse provides unwavering solace to every believer who has suffered worldly loss for the sake of religious principle. To turn down an unethical financial transaction, to lose business opportunities due to prayer, or to spend hard-earned capital on zakāh can feel daunting in an aggressive economy.

Verse 38 stands as an unshakeable divine guarantee that no servant will ever be impoverished by putting God first. The All-Bountiful Provider sees every sacrifice made in secret, and He guarantees that the final balance sheet will end in overwhelming victory. In both the provisional realms of this temporary world and the permanent abodes of the afterlife, those who trusted in God's bounty will find themselves enriched with treasures that decay can never destroy, basking in everlasting peace, divine approval, radiant honor, unceasing joy, and eternal fulfillment in the company of the righteous.

---
## Sūrah an-Nūr 24:39

> As for the disbelievers, their deeds are like a mirage in a desert, which the thirsty perceive as water, but when they approach it, they find it to be nothing. Instead, they find Allah there ˹in the Hereafter, ready˺ to settle their account. And Allah is swift in reckoning.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Dramatic Shift: From Luminous Reality to Desert Delusion**

The revelation executes a dramatic pedagogical contrast: having illuminated the radiant pathway of believers whose hearts are niches of divine light (vv. 35–38), the text turns abruptly to the tragic reality of disbelief: *wa-alladhīna kafarū aʿmāluhum ka-sarābin bi-qīʿah*. Disbelief is not merely a lack of intellectual assent; it is an ontological dislocation from truth that invalidates human exertion.

The verse addresses the ostensible "good deeds" performed by disbelievers—their philanthropic donations, scientific discoveries, civic building projects, and acts of social compassion. While these actions appear noble, impressive, and beneficial on the surface of temporal history, they lack the foundational anchor of divine faith. Without connection to the transcendent Creator, human deeds become weightless phantoms, devoid of eternal substance and doomed to evaporate like desert mist when confronted with ultimate reality, leaving the soul impoverished and spiritually barren.

**Anatomy of the Desert Mirage: ka-sarābin bi-qīʿatin**

The Qur'an selects an exquisite natural phenomenon to illustrate the tragic illusion of unanchored deeds: *ka-sarābin bi-qīʿatin*. The *sarāb* (mirage) occurs on flat, sun-baked desert plains (*qīʿah*), where intense thermal gradients refract light rays upward, creating the deceptive optical appearance of shimmering lakes and expansive bodies of cool water.

The physics of the mirage mirrors the moral psychology of the disbeliever. The mirage exists purely as a subjective optical illusion in the observer's mind, possessing zero objective physical reality. Similarly, the disbeliever flatters himself that his charitable works, cultural achievements, and worldly legacy will secure him salvation, redemption, or eternal remembrance. He gazes across the parched expanse of his mortal life and imagines that his self-congratulatory deeds form a reservoir of refreshing merit that will quench his spiritual thirst in the Hereafter, mistaking atmospheric tricks and optical illusions for enduring substance, grounding his hopes in baseless vanity.

**The Shattered Expectation of Thirst: yaḥsabuhu al-ẓamʾānu māʾā**

The human pathos of the parable is heightened by the traveler's desperation: *yaḥsabuhu al-ẓamʾānu māʾan ḥattā idhā jāʾahū lam yajidhu shayʾā*. The traveler is *ẓamʾān*—dying of agonizing, unquenchable thirst in a barren wilderness where survival depends entirely on reaching water.

Driven by desperate hope, the exhausted traveler expends his remaining physical energy stumbling toward the shimmering lake. Yet, as he draws closer, the optical illusion dissolves before his eyes, leaving only burning sand and mocking heat: *lam yajidhu shayʾan*—he finds it to be utterly nothing! This captures the horrific moment of eschatological awakening for those who rejected God. At the moment of death and resurrection, when the soul experiences the ultimate, existential thirst for mercy, forgiveness, and deliverance, they will reach for their accumulated worldly deeds only to find them hollow, worthless dust blown away by cosmic reality, leaving them in utter ruin, bankruptcy, and inconsolable despair.

**The Inescapable Divine Reckoning: wa-wajada Allāha ʿindah**

The text delivers a terrifying theological reversal: *wa-wajada Allāha ʿindahū fa-waffāhu ḥisābah*. Expecting life-giving water, the disbelieving soul discovers that instead of finding their deeds, they find God Himself waiting to execute judgment!

The expression *wa-wajada Allāha ʿindah* (and he found Allah there) conveys absolute inescapability. Throughout his worldly existence, the disbeliever ignored God, denied His messengers, and acted as though the universe possessed no sovereign Lord. Yet at the end of the desert mirage, God is present as the supreme Judge. God pays him his reckoning in full (*fa-waffāhu ḥisābah*). Any temporal reward deserved for worldly good was already granted during mortal life in the form of health, fame, or wealth; in the Hereafter, stripped of faith, the soul faces the crushing weight of its unrepented rebellion and finds itself completely destitute before divine majesty, with nowhere left to flee.

**Cosmic Irony of Disbelieving Righteousness**

This parable exposes the profound absurdity of attempting to construct moral goodness independent of God. When an individual performs philanthropic deeds while openly denying or ignoring the Creator who provided them with hands to work, breath to live, and wealth to spend, their actions are poisoned by ultimate ingratitude and pride.

It is as if an impostor stole a king's royal treasury, distributed a few coins to beggars in the street, and then demanded the king bestow a crown upon him for his philanthropy! True righteousness begins with acknowledging the Source of all existence. Good deeds detached from faith are severed branches that wither inevitably. They may look green for a brief moment in the temporal marketplace, but they have no spiritual root system capable of sustaining them into eternity, exposing the hollow nature of godless morality and moral pretense that dissolves upon encountering absolute reality.

**Divine Velocity in Cosmic Accounting: sarīʿu al-ḥisāb**

The verse concludes with an ominous warning: *wa-Allāhu sarīʿu al-ḥisāb*, and Allah is swift in reckoning. The divine reckoning is not delayed by bureaucratic hesitation, investigative delays, or difficult calculations.

Classical commentators explained that God reckons the deeds of all creatures simultaneously in a single instant, just as He sustains and provides for all creatures at once without fatigue. The swiftness of reckoning also signifies that the transition from mortal life to divine judgment is shockingly rapid. The lifespan of a human being in this world is but a fleeting moment, and before the heedless traveler can even comprehend the tragedy of their choices, the desert road terminates, the mirage evaporates, and they stand naked before the swift, unerring justice of the Lord of the worlds, where no excuses remain, no appeals exist, and truth reigns supreme forever.

---
## Sūrah an-Nūr 24:40

> Or ˹their deeds are˺ like the darkness in a deep sea, covered by waves upon waves, topped by ˹dark˺ clouds. Darkness upon darkness! If one stretches out their hand, they can hardly see it. And whoever Allah does not bless with light will have no light!

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Abyssal Sea: Stratification of Profound Darkness**

The revelation presents an alternative, even more harrowing cosmic allegory: *aw ka-ẓulumātin fī baḥrin lujjiyyin yaghshāhu mawjun min fawqihi mawjun min fawqihi saḥāb*. While verse 39 depicted the active, deceptive works of the disbeliever who mistakenly anticipates reward (the desert mirage), verse 40 portrays the inner epistemic state of the disbeliever who is completely submerged in existential despair, skepticism, and nihilism.

The text plunges the reader into a *baḥr lujjiyy*—a fathomless, abyssal ocean depths where sunlight never penetrates. Modern oceanography confirms that solar light diminishes rapidly with depth; below two hundred meters, the ocean enters twilight, and below one thousand meters, it descends into absolute pitch darkness. The Qur'an uses this terrifying oceanic abyss to represent the soul that has severed its connection to divine revelation, trapped in a cold, suffocating abyss of existential alienation and profound spiritual blindness that extinguishes hope.

**Atmospheric and Oceanic Turbulence: mawjun min fawqihi mawj**

The darkness of this oceanic trench is compounded by multiple physical and meteorological barriers: *yaghshāhu mawjun min fawqihi mawjun min fawqihi saḥāb*, covered by waves upon waves, topped by dark clouds. This astonishing imagery describes an intricate three-tiered architecture of occlusion.

First, deep within the sea are internal waves that undulate along boundaries of differing water densities; second, upon the surface crash towering ocean waves driven by gales; third, above the surface hang thick, light-blocking storm clouds (*saḥāb*). Each layer progressively absorbs, scatters, and extinguishes the remaining photons of celestial light. Metaphorically, these tiers represent the multiple covers suffocating the disbelieving soul: the internal darkness of corrupted character, the turbulent emotional waves of uncontrolled worldly desires, and the overarching cloud of cultural pride and philosophical arrogance that completely blocks the light of divine truth, leaving the inner eye starved of vision.

**Compounded Blindness: ẓulumātun baʿḍuhā fawqa baʿḍ**

The culmination of these obscuring layers is captured in an emphatic exclamation: *ẓulumātun baʿḍuhā fawqa baʿḍ*, darknesses, one above the other! This phrase stands in direct, breathtaking antithesis to the proclamation in verse 35: *nūrun ʿalā nūr* (Light upon light!).

Just as divine illumination compounds upon itself through pure human intuition, sacred scripture, and righteous deeds, spiritual blindness compounds upon itself through doubt, transgression, and willful obstinacy. The classical companion Ubayy ibn Kaʿb commented that the disbeliever is trapped in five darknesses: his speech is dark, his actions are dark, his entrance into the grave is dark, his resurrection is dark, and his ultimate destiny in the Fire is dark. It is a total, suffocating occlusion of spiritual perception that leaves no room for truth to enter, transforming the soul into a frozen landscape of negation.

**The Vanishing Extremity: idhā akhraja yadahu lam yakad yarāhā**

The psychological horror of this absolute darkness is made visceral through human physical action: *idhā akhraja yadahu lam yakad yarāhā*, when one puts out his hand, he can hardly see it. A person's hand is the closest, most familiar part of their own physical anatomy; if a person cannot even perceive their own hand held directly before their eyes, all navigational orientation has ceased to exist.

This image symbolizes the total loss of moral and existential orientation. The disbeliever who rejects divine revelation cannot even understand his own human nature, his purpose, or his ultimate destination. He stumbles through life in complete moral disorientation, unable to perceive the consequences of his actions or recognize the evil taking shape in his own hands. His self-proclaimed philosophical sophistication is exposed as pathetic, helpless blindness that cannot guide a single footstep across the dark terrain of mortality.

**The Anatomy of Compound Ignorance: al-jahl al-murakkab**

Classical Islamic theologians and epistemologists analyzed this verse as the definitive Qur'anic diagnosis of compound ignorance (*al-jahl al-murakkab*). Simple ignorance (*al-jahl al-basīṭ*) occurs when an individual does not know a truth but is consciously aware of their lack of knowledge, leaving them receptive to instruction.

In contrast, compound ignorance is a catastrophic spiritual disease: the person does not know the truth, does not know that they do not know, and fervently believes that they possess superior wisdom! Like the submerged swimmer beneath layers of storm clouds and oceanic waves who imagines their darkness is the entire universe, the arrogant skeptic ridicules faith while residing in profound intellectual decay. They are blind to their own blindness, trapped in a self-enclosed prison of philosophical confusion from which no human logic alone can liberate them, mistaking shadows for enlightenment.

**The Sovereign Dispenser of Spiritual Vision: man lam yajʿali Allāhu lahū nūrā**

The verse culminates in an absolute, unyielding metaphysical verdict: *wa-man lam yajʿali Allāhu lahū nūran fa-mā lahū min nūr*, and whoever Allah does not bless with light will have no light! This divine sentence settles the ultimate source of truth, insight, and salvation.

Light is not an autonomous human achievement that can be manufactured through secular philosophy, intellectual brilliance, or technological mastery. God is the sole Creator and Sovereign Bestower of light (*al-Muʿṭī lil-nūr*). If a soul rejects God's guidance, cuts itself off from His messengers, and chooses the dark depths of ego and skepticism, no power in the cosmos can provide it with illumination. It is doomed to stumble through the darkness of mortality into the eternal shadows of the afterlife. True vision is a divine grace, granted to the humble heart that turns toward the Light of the heavens and the earth, finding eternal guidance, serenity, and deliverance in sacred revelation across all realms.

---
## Sūrah an-Nūr 24:41

> Do you not see that Allah is glorified by all those in the heavens and the earth, even the birds as they soar? Each ˹instinctively˺ knows their manner of prayer and glorification. And Allah has ˹perfect˺ knowledge of all they do.

**Expanded Commentary**

**Cosmic Liturgy: yusabbiḥu lahū man fī al-samāwāt**

The divine speech opens with an awakening rhetorical question: *a-lam tara anna Allāha yusabbiḥu lahū man fī al-samāwāti wa-al-arḍ*, do you not see that Allah is glorified by all those in the heavens and the earth? The visual verb *tara* (do you not see) invites both sensory observation of the natural cosmos and deep intellectual reflection upon its underlying order and harmony.

The entire universe is presented not as a cold, inert mechanism of mute matter, but as an ecstatic, living sanctuary vibrating with sacred praise. Angels in celestial spheres, stars in galactic orbits, oceans, mountains, trees, and microscopic organisms all participate in continuous glorification (*tasbīḥ*). This universal chorus affirms the absolute transcendence, perfection, and purity of the Creator, declaring Him free from every partner, deficiency, or mortal weakness. Every created atom exists in an ontological posture of praise before its Maker, glorifying the Sovereign of all realms whose wisdom sustains every corner of existence and whose beauty fills the horizons.

**Aerial Hymns: wa-al-ṭayru ṣāffāt**

The verse highlights a particularly breathtaking manifestation of terrestrial worship: *wa-al-ṭayru ṣāffāt*, even the birds as they soar with outspread wings. The word *ṣāffāt* depicts birds gliding high in the sky, extending their wings in effortless aerial suspension without frantic flapping, sustained entirely by divine aerodynamic decrees that govern the atmosphere.

Classical commentators, including al-Qurṭubī, al-Ṭabarī, and al-Rāzī, noted that the outstretched wings of soaring birds mirror the outspread arms and bowed postures of human worshippers in prayer. As birds glide through the atmosphere, their rhythmic ascent and aerial maneuvers constitute their own distinctive liturgy of adoration. Their flight is an open sign of complete dependence upon the Sustainer (*al-Qayyūm*), who holds them aloft in the thin air, transforming the skies into a soaring temple of praise where every flight path testifies to divine balance and providence, inspiring awe in human onlookers.

**Distinction Between Prayer and Glorification: kullun qad ʿalima ṣalātahu wa-tasbīḥah**

The text establishes a profound semantic and operational distinction: *kullun qad ʿalima ṣalātahu wa-tasbīḥah*, each instinctively knows its manner of prayer and glorification. While *tasbīḥ* (glorification) is the declaration of divine transcendence and freedom from flaw, *ṣalāh* (prayer) denotes supplication, dynamic posture, and active communion with the Maker.

Early exegete al-Ṭabarī noted that while some scholars understood *ṣalāh* to refer specifically to human and angelic liturgy and *tasbīḥ* to non-human creation, the syntax affirms that each distinct species possesses both its own specific mode of prayer and its own method of praise. The soaring bird has its *ṣalāh* in the beating and gliding of its wings, and its *tasbīḥ* in its dawn melodies and instinctive calls. Creation does not offer a chaotic cacophony; it performs an orderly, differentiated cosmic symphony wherein every species faithfully executes its ordained liturgical assignment, weaving a grand harmony of adoration.

**Instinctive Knowledge and Primordial Endowments**

The phrase *kullun qad ʿalima* indicates that this worship does not arise from external compulsion or mechanical programming; it is rooted in an innate divine knowledge granted to every creature. God has inspired every animal, bird, and celestial body with the knowledge of how to orient its existence toward its Creator.

This intuitive orientation (*al-ilhām al-fiṭrī*) bypasses human linguistic formulations. A bird gliding effortlessly across the thermal currents does not compose theological treatises, yet its very flight pattern, biological design, and joyful calls express total, harmonious alignment with divine will. Every creature recognizes its Maker and responds with appropriate reverence according to the cognitive and physical faculties bestowed upon it, displaying a pristine integrity that puts human heedlessness to shame, functioning as a silent sermon directed toward mortal hearts, calling them back to natural submission.

**The Tragic Dissonance of Human Heedlessness**

Contemplating this universal chorus of praise exposes the tragic anomaly of human and jinn disbelief. In a cosmos where galaxies bow, mountains glorify, and soaring birds sing anthems to their Creator, the disbelieving human being stands out as a jarring, discordant note.

Endowed with intellect, free will, and moral agency, humanity was created to be the conscious crown of terrestrial worship. Yet, many human beings descend into arrogant denial, trivial distraction, or insolent rebellion, refusing to bow the head that God elevated. While birds greet the dawn with hymns of gratitude, heedless mortals slumber past prayer or awaken to pursue petty worldly greed. The verse gently rebukes this spiritual deafness, inviting human beings to abandon their unnatural estrangement and harmonize their hearts with the vast cosmic choir that ceaselessly honors God, reclaiming their true station as humble guardians of creation who live in conscious gratitude.

**Divine Surveillance of Cosmic Devotion: Allāhu ʿalīmun bimā yafʿalūn**

The verse concludes with a comprehensive declaration of divine omniscience: *wa-Allāhu ʿalīmun bimā yafʿalūn*, and Allah has perfect knowledge of all they do. The verb *yafʿalūn* (they do) encompasses both the voluntary and instinctive actions of all creatures.

God does not merely hear the audible prayers of humans; He knows the silent flutter of the sparrow's wing, the migratory flight path of the swallow, and the secret stirrings of devotion within every created heart. Nothing in this expansive cosmic sanctuary escapes His attention or goes unrecorded. For the righteous, this divine knowledge provides profound comfort, assuring them that their quiet prayers in elevated houses are seen and cherished by the same Lord who orchestrates the praise of heavens, earth, and sky, giving them patience to persevere in virtue until the day they meet Him, welcomed into everlasting peace.

---
## Sūrah an-Nūr 24:42

> To Allah ˹alone˺ belongs the kingdom of the heavens and the earth. And to Allah is the final return.

**Expanded Commentary**

**Sovereign Dominion over Heaven and Earth: wa-lillāhi mulku al-samāwāt**

The divine discourse follows the contemplation of cosmic worship (v. 41) with an absolute statement of constitutional authority: *wa-lillāhi mulku al-samāwāti wa-al-arḍ*, to Allah alone belongs the kingdom of the heavens and the earth. The syntax employs the rhetorical device of *taqdīm* (fronting the prepositional phrase *lillāh*), establishing strict exclusivity: dominion belongs to God alone, without partner, rival, advisor, or successor.

The term *mulk* encompasses total ownership, supreme political sovereignty, legislative authority, and cosmological governance across all dimensions of time and space. Every celestial galaxy, planetary system, atmospheric layer, terrestrial continent, and biological entity exists as part of His undisputed realm. He regulates physical laws with flawless consistency and ordains moral statutes with ultimate justice, providing sustenance to every creature. No king, emperor, or legislative assembly holds genuine autonomous sovereignty; whatever power human beings exercise on earth is merely a provisional, heavily circumscribed trusteeship granted temporarily by the King of kings, who alone governs life, death, resurrection, and judgment, commanding eternal reverence and obedience.

**Illusory Ownership versus Transcendent Authority**

Human beings routinely fall victim to the psychological delusion of autonomous ownership. When an individual purchases land, builds a palatial residence, amasses financial investments, or ascends to political office, their ego whispers that they have become absolute masters of their domain.

Verse 42 radically shatters this prideful illusion. The earth beneath our feet, the atmosphere we inhale, the physical bodies we inhabit, and the material wealth we manage belong entirely to God. Human ownership is temporary, conditional, and strictly accountable before divine law at every stage of mortal life. When a landowner dies, their deeds, titles, and legal certificates dissolve into irrelevance; the earth remains, quietly outliving its boastful claimants. By anchoring human consciousness in God's exclusive ownership, the Qur'an cures the soul of avarice, tyranny, and arrogance, instilling a humble ethic of responsible stewardship (*khilāfah*) over divine property, freeing the heart from possessiveness and destructive materialism.

**The Terminal Convergence: wa-ilā Allāhi al-maṣīr**

The verse pairs divine sovereignty over space with absolute authority over time and destiny: *wa-ilā Allāhi al-maṣīr*, and to Allah is the final return. The noun *maṣīr* derives from the root *ṣ-y-r*, signifying transformation, destination, and the ultimate terminal outcome toward which all moving things converge.

Every created journey—whether the life trajectory of an individual human being, the historical arc of an empire, or the cosmic lifespan of the universe—inevitably terminates at the divine throne. No soul can escape beyond the cosmic perimeter of God's dominion, nor can any rebel evade the appointed meeting. The trajectory of existence is not an endless, meaningless cycle of biological birth and decay, nor a linear drift into eternal nothingness. It is an intentional, purposeful journey culminating in divine audience and total accountability, giving ultimate moral seriousness, dignity, and eternal weight to mortal existence.

**Ontological Reassurance for the Righteous**

For the vulnerable believer striving to maintain righteousness amidst a hostile, corrupt, or intimidating environment, verse 42 provides profound psychological comfort, courage, and emotional security. When tyrannical powers abuse their authority or worldly corruption appears overwhelmingly dominant, the believer remembers that the kingdom belongs solely to God, not to mortal despots.

Earthly tyrants do not own the heavens and the earth; they are merely temporary dust walking upon God's earth, breathing God's air, and hurtling toward God's judgment seat. Their arrogant dictates cannot alter divine decree, nor can their persecution outlast the brief span of mortal history. Knowing that *al-maṣīr* is toward the All-Just Lord empowers the righteous to stand firm with unyielding dignity, refusing to compromise their moral integrity out of fear or worldly desperation, knowing that absolute justice awaits all souls in the divine presence, where every wrong is righted.

**Dismantling the Delusions of Earthly Empire**

The historical resonance of this verse was immediately felt by the early Muslim community facing the formidable empires of Persia and Rome. To the eyes of worldly observers, these superpower empires possessed invincible armies, vast treasuries, and unshakeable political dominance that commanded global obedience and submission from weaker nations.

Verse 42 swept aside this intimidating aura with effortless divine authority. In the eyes of the Qur'an, imperial thrones are transient shadows; true sovereignty (*al-mulk*) belongs exclusively to the Creator. History repeatedly validates this Qur'anic perspective: mighty empires that once dominated continents now lie buried beneath desert sands, their palaces reduced to shattered ruins, their proud inscriptions undecipherable without archeological excavation. Only the living King survives, reigning supreme over creation long after earthly crowns have crumbled into dust, proving the utter vanity of mortal power.

**Cosmic Accountability as the Horizon of Human Action**

Sealing the verse with *wa-ilā Allāhi al-maṣīr* establishes an inescapable horizon that shapes every ethical decision and colors every moral choice. If every human path leads inexorably back to God, then all earthly transactions, political policies, domestic interactions, and personal choices must be calibrated against that final meeting.

A person who knows they must return to their Maker cannot exploit an orphan, defraud a business partner, slander a chaste neighbor, or live in heedless sensual indulgence. The reality of *al-maṣīr* transforms ethics from external social etiquette into an urgent existential imperative that shapes character and guides conduct. Every step taken on earth is a step closer to the Creator. Wise servants structure their fleeting days around the single question: How will this deed appear when I present it before the Sovereign Lord on the Day of the final return, when all earthly secrets are unveiled, excuses vanish, and divine truth reigns supreme throughout eternity, rewarding righteousness with everlasting peace and radiant honor before all creation?

---
## Sūrah an-Nūr 24:43

> Do you not see that Allah gently drives the clouds, then joins them together, piling them up into masses, from which you see raindrops come forth? And He sends down from the sky mountains ˹of clouds˺ loaded with hail, pouring it on whoever He wills and averting it from whoever He wills. The flash of the clouds’ lightning nearly takes away eyesight.

**Expanded Commentary**

**Dynamic Meteorology: yuzjī saḥāban**

The Qur'an directs human contemplation toward the dynamic, awe-inspiring processes of atmospheric physics: *a-lam tara anna Allāha yuzjī saḥāban thumma yuʾallifu baynahū thumma yajʿaluhū rukāmā*. The verb *yuzjī* depicts gentle, gradual driving; winds are steered by divine decree to push isolated, scattered fragments of water vapor across vast atmospheric expanses.

Modern meteorological science reveals that cloud formation begins precisely with this gentle movement: wind currents herd cumulus clouds into convergence zones across great oceanic and continental expanses. The subsequent phase, *thumma yuʾallifu baynah* (then He joins them together), describes how micro-clouds coalesce, combining their thermal updrafts and moisture reserves into unified structures. Divine providence operates through precise physical mechanisms, choreographing invisible air masses, temperatures, barometric pressures, and moisture levels to initiate the complex cycle of terrestrial hydration that sustains life across continents, displaying unmatched creative power and engineering perfection in every gentle breeze.

**Cumulonimbus Architecture: yajʿaluhu rukāman**

The text describes the third stage of cloud development with striking accuracy: *thumma yajʿaluhū rukāmā*, then piling them up into dense masses. The noun *rukām* signifies vertical stacking, layering, and condensation into towering heaps.

In modern atmospheric science, this corresponds to the development of massive cumulonimbus clouds. When smaller cumulus clouds merge, powerful vertical updrafts carry moist air thousands of meters upward into the colder reaches of the troposphere. The cloud structure expands vertically, piling mass upon mass until it forms a gigantic, towering anvil-shaped mountain of condensed moisture. This vertical piling is essential for cloud dynamics, enabling the condensation, collision, and coalescence of billions of microscopic water droplets into heavy raindrops capable of falling through turbulent updrafts to reach the thirsty ground below, nourishing vegetation, sustaining wildlife, and replenishing vital springs that feed entire human communities.

**Emergence of Life-Giving Rain: yakhruju min khilālih**

The verse traces the miraculous consequence of this atmospheric architecture: *fa-tarā al-wadqa yakhruju min khilālih*, from which you see raindrops come forth. The term *al-wadq* specifically designates gentle, penetrating raindrops emerging from the interior recesses (*khilāl*) of the cloud mass.

Rain does not fall as a catastrophic, crushing solid sheet of water that would destroy vegetation, scour topsoil, and crush human dwellings. Instead, divine wisdom engineers atmospheric resistance, air friction, and surface tension so that moisture falls as billions of measured, distinct droplets. Emerging from the very heart of dark, towering storm clouds, this measured rain revives parched landscapes, fills underground aquifers, feeds roaring rivers, and germinates sleeping seeds, demonstrating how divine mercy emerges out of seeming darkness to sustain life across the globe, transforming barren wilderness into lush gardens of abundant provision, beauty, and renewal.

**Atmospheric Mountains and Catastrophic Hail: jibālin fīhā min barad**

The discourse ascends to the violent, frigid heights of the storm cloud: *wa-yunazzilu mina al-samāʾi min jibālin fīhā min barad*, and He sends down from the sky mountains loaded with hail. Classical exegetes noted two profound interpretations of *jibāl* (mountains).

First, looking upward at cumulonimbus clouds, or looking down upon them from aircraft, they present the literal visual appearance of immense, snow-capped mountain ranges rising miles into the sky. Second, inside these atmospheric "mountains," supercooled water droplets circulate through violent vertical currents, freezing onto ice pellets layer by layer to form destructive hail (*barad*). When these frozen missiles plummet to earth, they possess formidable destructive force, capable of flattening crops, shattering roofs, injuring livestock, and devastating agricultural economies in minutes, revealing the dual nature of weather as both mercy and trial, warning human hearts of latent power and reminding them of divine majesty.

**Divine Sovereignty over Blessings and Afflictions: yuṣību bihi man yashāʾ**

The distribution of weather phenomena is governed by precise divine sovereignty: *fa-yuṣību bihī man yashāʾu wa-yaṣrifuhū ʿan man yashāʾ*, pouring it on whoever He wills and averting it from whoever He wills. In a single valley, hail may obliterate the orchards of one village while completely sparing neighboring fields just meters away.

Meteorologists recognize the extreme localized nature of hailstorms, which often travel in narrow, erratic swaths determined by micro-climatic turbulence. The Qur'an elevates this physical reality to spiritual awareness: weather is never an indifferent, mechanical accident. God employs atmospheric phenomena as instruments of both mercy and testing. Rain brings fertility and wealth to one community, while localized hail brings financial hardship or divine warning to another, reminding humanity of its absolute vulnerability and total dependence upon divine grace for daily survival, food security, and economic stability.

**The Blinding Glare of Cosmic Electricity: yakādu sanā barqih**

The verse culminates in an intense sensory climax: *yakādu sanā barqihi yadhhabu bi-al-abṣār*, the flash of the clouds' lightning nearly takes away eyesight. Within the towering cumulonimbus mountain, violent collisions between ice crystals and supercooled water generate massive electrical charges.

When these colossal charges discharge as lightning bolts, they unleash blinding flashes of light (*sanā barqih*) that illuminate the dark heavens with terrifying brilliance and thunderous power. The overwhelming flash temporarily overpowers human retinal receptors, threatening total blindness and shattering night darkness. Symbolically, this lightning flash mirrors the terrifying, incandescent majesty of divine revelation when it strikes the darkness of the world. Divine truth is so bright, so pure, and so intense that those unprepared for its brilliance are overwhelmed, while those with guided vision see by its flash the path leading to eternal salvation, moving through life with awe, profound humility, and unshakeable certainty beneath the shelter of divine grace.

---
## Sūrah an-Nūr 24:44

> Allah alternates the day and night. Surely in this is a lesson for people of insight.

**Expanded Commentary**

**Celestial Cadence and Planetary Rotation: yuqallibu Allāhu al-layla wa-al-nahār**

The divine speech directs human attention to the perpetual cosmic rhythm governing terrestrial existence: *yuqallibu Allāhu al-layla wa-al-nahār*, Allah alternates the day and night. The verb *yuqallib* conveys continuous turning, rotating, alternating, and transforming.

This single Arabic verb encapsulates complex astronomical phenomena: the axial rotation of the earth presenting alternating faces to the solar furnace, the gradual elongation and shortening of diurnal hours across seasonal solstices and equinoxes, and the atmospheric transition between radiant daylight and nocturnal stillness. This alternation is neither random nor static. It is a dynamic, mathematically calibrated celestial dance choreographed by the Creator, ensuring that temperatures remain habitable, atmospheric cycles function smoothly, and planetary life flourishes within stable cosmic rhythms, revealing divine mercy and supreme architectural design.

**Cosmic Balance and the Rhythm of Existence**

The alternation of day and night provides the biological and psychological architecture necessary for human life. If the earth were locked in permanent daylight, the relentless heat, glare, and ceaseless sensory stimulation would desiccate terrestrial ecosystems and drive human minds into neurological exhaustion and emotional instability.

Conversely, if the planet were cast into perpetual night, freezing temperatures would obliterate agriculture and paralyze human civilization in freezing gloom. Through divine alternation (*taqlīb*), the day is ordained as an expansive field for productive labor, economic enterprise, and intellectual striving, while the night descends as a cool, protective sanctuary for physiological restoration, emotional intimacy, and tranquil contemplation. This ceaseless polarity balances exertion with rest, action with silence, and external endeavor with interior renewal, allowing human communities to maintain physical vitality and spiritual equilibrium.

**The Epistemology of Perception: inna fī dhālika la-ʿibrah**

The verse declares the pedagogical essence of this astronomical cycle: *inna fī dhālika la-ʿibratan*, surely in this is an instructive lesson. The noun *ʿibrah* derives from the root *ʿ-b-r*, meaning to cross over, traverse, or bridge from one bank of a river to the other.

In Qur'anic epistemology, an *ʿibrah* is an empirical observation that serves as a conceptual bridge, enabling human consciousness to cross over from the visible realm of material effects (*ʿālam al-shahādah*) to the invisible realm of transcendent divine realities (*ʿālam al-ghayb*). When a person witnesses the arrival of dusk, the emergence of the stars, and the subsequent rebirth of morning light, they are not merely observing photons and planetary physics. They are gazing upon a profound existential sign demonstrating that behind physical phenomena reigns an intentional, living, and omnipotent Lord who orders existence with supreme wisdom and unceasing care.

**The Requisite Quality of Vision: li-ulī al-abṣār**

The divine text specifies who is capable of crossing this cognitive bridge: *li-ulī al-abṣār*, for people of insight. The term *abṣār* is the plural of *baṣar*, which denotes both physical eyesight and the interior perceptual faculty of the heart (*baṣīrah*).

Millions of people witness the sunrise and sunset every single day of their lives, yet they perceive nothing beyond routine mechanics or aesthetic novelty. Their physical eyes function with optical precision, but their spiritual vision is entirely blind; they consume the light of day without recognizing the Light that created it. In contrast, the people of true insight (*ulū al-abṣār*) possess an awakened heart that penetrates beyond surface appearances. They view every sunrise as a triumphant proclamation of resurrection, and every sunset as a solemn reminder of mortality, translating physical sights into spiritual elevation, awe, and moral vigilance.

**The Transience of Human Fortune in the Mirror of Time**

Beyond astronomy, the alternation of night and day (*taqlīb*) serves as a profound metaphor for the volatility of human circumstances. Just as brilliant noon inevitably surrenders to creeping dusk, and pitch midnight is inevitably pierced by morning dawn, human life alternates constantly between joy and sorrow, health and sickness, wealth and poverty, power and vulnerability.

No worldly condition is permanent. An arrogant ruler enjoying the bright noon of political hegemony can find his empire plunged into the dark night of ruin before the sun sets. Conversely, the oppressed believer enduring the cold midnight of trial can trust that the dawn of divine relief (*faraj*) is drawing near. Recognizing this cosmic principle cures the human ego of two destructive diseases: complacency during times of prosperity, and despair during times of affliction, anchoring the soul in patient steadfastness, dignified humility, and unshakeable hope in divine justice.

**Harmonizing the Human Heart with the Cosmic Cycle**

The ultimate spiritual invitation of verse 44 is to harmonize human life with the celestial cadence established by God. The alternation of night and day was engineered to align with the rhythms of worship revealed in sacred law.

The faithful servant greets the first glimmer of dawn with the Fajr prayer, pauses in the heat of noon for Ẓuhr, acknowledges the declining sun at ʿAṣr, marks the sunset at Maghrib, and welcomes the night with ʿIshāʾ. In this way, human consciousness never drifts into heedlessness for more than a few hours at a time. The passing of time is not mourned as a tragic loss of youth or feared as an approaching void, but celebrated as a purposeful journey through stages of divine praise, moving the soul closer each day to its eternal Lord, transforming the ticking clock into deliberate steps toward eternal salvation and celestial peace.

The alternation of light and darkness also mirrors the spiritual journey of the believer, who experiences seasons of radiant spiritual expansion followed by periods of sobering contraction. Just as the night serves an indispensable biological function in cooling the earth and allowing roots to absorb moisture, spiritual trials and quiet nights of contemplation deepen the soul's roots in humility and reliance upon God. Those possessed of true insight perceive that both day and night, joy and sorrow, ease and difficulty, are coordinated by the same loving Creator to bring the human spirit to ultimate spiritual maturity.

---
## Sūrah an-Nūr 24:45

> And Allah has created from water every living creature. Some of them crawl on their bellies, some walk on two legs, and some walk on four. Allah creates whatever He wills. Surely Allah is Most Capable of everything.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Primordial Matrix: khalaqa kulla dābbatin min māʾ**

The revelation directs human inquiry into the biological foundation of terrestrial life: *wa-Allāhu khalaqa kulla dābbatin min māʾ*, and Allah has created from water every living creature. The noun *dābbah* designates any sentient, ambulating creature that moves across the earth's surface.

The assertion that all living creatures originate from water articulates a profound biological and chemical truth. Modern cellular biology confirms that water (*H2O*) constitutes between sixty to ninety percent of the cellular cytoplasm in all living organisms, serving as the indispensable universal solvent without which biochemical reactions, nutrient transport, metabolic synthesis, and genetic replication are physically impossible. Furthermore, classical exegetes like al-Rāzī noted that *māʾ* also refers to reproductive fluids that initiate fertilization and embryonic development. Water is both the primordial matrix from which life emerged and the continuous biological fluid that sustains every living cell on earth.

**Diverse Locomotion: Crawlers, Bipeds, and Quadrupeds**

From this uniform chemical foundation, the text highlights the astonishing diversity of animal locomotion: *fa-minhum man yamshī ʿalā baṭnih wa-minhum man yamshī ʿalā rijlayn wa-minhum man yamshī ʿalā arbaʿ*. Despite sharing an identical elemental origin in water, living creatures diverge into radically different anatomical forms and locomotive mechanics.

First, there are creatures that glide or crawl upon their bellies (*man yamshī ʿalā baṭnih*), such as snakes, legless lizards, worms, and mollusks, utilizing rhythmic muscular contractions and specialized scales to navigate subterranean burrows, rocky crevices, and tree canopies. Second, there are bipeds (*man yamshī ʿalā rijlayn*), including humans and birds, balancing their upright weight on two limbs, freeing hands for tool use or transforming forelimbs into aerodynamic wings. Third, there are quadrupeds (*man yamshī ʿalā arbaʿ*), including beasts of burden, predators, and grazing herds, whose four-legged anatomy provides terrestrial speed, load-bearing endurance, and environmental stability across vast landscapes.

**Anatomical Differentiation as Divine Artistry**

The morphological variety of terrestrial fauna presents an insurmountable challenge to purely materialist assumptions of blind chance. If life arose from unguided accidental collisions of matter within a primordial soup, one would expect either uniform, monotonous biological structures or chaotic, unviable monstrosities.

Instead, the animal kingdom exhibits breathtaking, purposeful morphological variety. The skeletal frameworks, musculature, neurological networks, and sensory organs of crawlers, bipeds, and quadrupeds are each exquisitely calibrated for their respective ecological niches. The serpent moves silently through narrow fissures inaccessible to quadrupeds; the bird ascends into aerial freedom; the human walks upright with an elevated gaze toward the cosmos. Uniformity of chemical origin paired with boundless functional diversity points unerringly to an imaginative, conscious, and transcendent Designer who crafts living forms with supreme aesthetic and utilitarian brilliance.

**Sovereign Creative Liberty: yakhluqu Allāhu mā yashāʾ**

The verse explains the philosophical cause behind this biological diversity: *yakhluqu Allāhu mā yashāʾ*, Allah creates whatever He wills. Creation is not constrained by deterministic necessity, mechanical algorithms, or rigid natural laws that bind the Creator.

The laws of physics and biology are themselves creations of God, sustained by His active will and subject to His sovereign discretion. God is not an impersonal first cause who set an automated clockwork universe in motion only to withdraw into cosmic detachment. He is the Living Creator (*al-Khāliq*) who continuously innovates, designs, and actualizes whatever forms He chooses. Mentioning crawling, two-legged, and four-legged creatures serves as a representative taxonomy that hints at still greater wonders—creatures with six legs, eight legs, or centipedes with dozens of limbs, as well as marine creatures and invisible microorganisms that populate every ecological sphere.

**The Teleology of Biological Complexity**

Contemplating the diverse locomotive faculties of animals reveals an integrated ecological teleology that directly serves human civilization. Quadrupeds provide transportation, agricultural traction, meat, wool, and milk, enabling human communities to cultivate soil, transport trade goods across continents, and establish stable civilizations.

Bipeds among birds contribute to pest control, seed dispersal, and pollination, while their soaring flight inspires human aspirations for exploration. Even creatures crawling upon their bellies serve vital ecological roles, aerating soil, decomposing organic matter, and maintaining predator-prey equilibria in delicate biomes. Humanity occupies a uniquely dignified position within this zoological order: created upright on two legs, endowed with rational intellect, moral agency, and spiritual accountability, man is appointed not to exploit or abuse fellow creatures, but to act as a benevolent, responsible steward over the living trust placed into his hands by the Creator.

The diversity of locomotion also demonstrates the limitless range of divine mercy in equipping each creature with the exact physiological apparatus necessary for its survival and sustenance. A snake gliding silently through dry sand is furnished with sensory organs capable of detecting infrared radiation; a camel striding across arid dunes is equipped with specialized footpads that prevent sinking into soft sand and eyelashes that protect against fierce desert dust storms; an eagle soaring above mountain peaks possesses telescopic visual acuity to locate prey from thousands of feet in the air. Every physical feature stands as clear proof of purposeful engineering, destroying any rational basis for attributing the living world to random chance.

**Divine Omnipotence over Natural Law: ʿalā kulli shayʾin Qadīr**

The verse seals its biological exposition with a definitive cosmological formula: *inna Allāha ʿalā kulli shayʾin qadīr*, surely Allah is Most Capable of everything. The divine attribute *al-Qadīr* denotes absolute, untrammeled omnipotence.

He who fashioned living, breathing, intelligent beings out of a humble drop of lifeless water possesses the effortless power to resurrect decayed bones, reconstruct decomposed bodies, and call all mortal beings to account on the Day of Judgment. The skeptics who doubt the physical resurrection are invited to gaze upon their own biological origin: if God initiated life from stagnant dust and fluid water in the first creation, replicating that creation in the afterlife is infinitely effortless for Him. Biological observation thus transitions seamlessly into eschatological certainty, leaving the thinking mind in reverent submission before the Lord of all living forms.

---
## Sūrah an-Nūr 24:46

> We have indeed sent down revelations clarifying ˹the truth˺. But Allah ˹only˺ guides whoever He wills to the Straight Path.

**Expanded Commentary**

**Clarifying Signs: āyātin mubayyināt**

The divine voice reasserts the clarity and efficacy of its revealed guidance: *la-qad anzalnā āyātin mubayyināt*, We have indeed sent down revelations clarifying the truth. The term *mubayyināt* emphasizes active, radiant illumination that leaves no room for moral ambiguity.

These verses do not merely possess internal coherence; they actively illuminate the landscape of human existence, dissipating the fog of moral ambiguity, superstition, and ethical confusion that routinely misleads societies. Throughout Sūrah an-Nūr, the revelation has systematically articulated the principles of domestic sanctuary, personal modesty, social chastity, legal evidence, and civic justice, establishing an unshakeable moral framework for society. Having just directed the human gaze to the cosmic signs—the soaring birds, the mountainous cumulonimbus clouds, the alternate rhythm of day and night, and the watery genesis of living creatures (vv. 41–45)—the text unites celestial observation with scriptural revelation, asserting that both books of God, nature and scripture, speak with undeniable clarity and compelling authority.

**The Dual Nature of Guidance: Universal Sign vs. Special Favor**

A crucial theological distinction emerges in the contrast between the first and second halves of the verse: *wa-Allāhu yahdī man yashāʾu ilā ṣirāṭin mustaqīm*, but Allah only guides whoever He wills to the Straight Path. The revelation itself is sent down universally to all humanity (*anzalnā ilaykum*), accessible to any reader or listener without restriction.

However, the actual attainment of transformative guidance (*al-hidāyah*) is an exclusive divine favor granted according to divine wisdom and human sincerity. Classical theologians, including al-Rāzī, al-Ṭabarī, and al-Bayḍāwī, differentiated between two levels of guidance: *hidāyat al-dalālah* (the external pointing out of the path through revealed scripture and prophetic instruction, which is universal) and *hidāyat al-tawfīq* (the internal divine facilitation that opens the heart, illuminates the conscience, and grants steadfastness upon the truth). While all human beings possess access to the clarifying signs, only those who approach the revelation with humility and moral sincerity receive the inner illumination necessary to traverse the Straight Path safely.

**The Straight Path: ilā ṣirāṭin mustaqīm**

The destination toward which divine guidance directs the receptive soul is *ṣirāṭ mustaqīm*—the Straight Path. In classical Arabic semantics, a *ṣirāṭ* is a wide, unobstructed highway that is completely straight, free of deviations, and leads directly to the desired destination without peril.

The qualifier *mustaqīm* denotes perfect vertical and horizontal rectitude; it neither bends toward sinful indulgence nor twists toward fanatical extremism. In the context of Sūrah an-Nūr, the Straight Path represents a balanced lifestyle where private sexual desires are harmoniously channeled into sacred marriage, communal relationships are protected from corrosive suspicion and slander, and commercial activities are conducted without neglecting spiritual devotions. It is the unswerving path of integral monotheism, where daily human conduct aligns completely with eternal transcendent values, leading to peace in this world, social stability, and eternal salvation in the hereafter, providing safe passage into the divine presence.

**Intellectual Reception versus Spiritual Transformation**

The juxtaposition of universally clarifying verses and selective spiritual guidance exposes the profound difference between intellectual comprehension and spiritual transformation. A brilliant philologist or skeptical academic may analyze the grammatical nuances, rhetorical structures, and legal codes of the Qur'an with astonishing accuracy, yet remain completely devoid of faith, trapped in cynicism.

Understanding the intellectual meaning of a verse is fundamentally distinct from surrendering the ego to its moral demands. The clarifying signs provide undeniable evidence, but if the human heart is infested with pride, cynicism, or unrepented moral corruption, the light of scripture cannot penetrate its calcified exterior. Divine guidance (*tawfīq*) descends only upon those souls who are willing to translate intellectual insight into existential obedience, allowing the divine word to shatter their self-centered illusions and reshape their life priorities in submission to their Lord, anchoring their will in divine pleasure.

**The Receptivity of the Humiliated Ego**

The clause *yahdī man yashāʾ* (He guides whoever He wills) is intimately tied to divine justice and human spiritual readiness. God does not arbitrarily deny guidance to sincere seekers, nor does He bestow it upon arrogant hypocrites who despise truth.

Throughout the Qur'anic corpus, divine will operates according to established spiritual laws: God guides those who turn to Him in repentance (*yahdī ilayhi man yunīb*), those who strive sincerely in His path (*wa-alladhīna jāhadū fīnā la-nahdiyannahum subulanā*), and those who possess humble, trembling hearts. Conversely, He turns away the arrogant, the corrupt, and the persistent deceivers who revel in their stubbornness. The soul that humbles itself, acknowledges its absolute dependency upon the Creator, and cries out for light is precisely the soul that divine grace selects for illumination, guiding its steps along the secure highway of truth through all earthly confusion, preserving it from spiritual ruin.

**The Bridge between Cosmic Wonder and Civic Ethics**

Verse 46 serves as a pivotal hinge within the architecture of Sūrah an-Nūr. It bridges the grand cosmological contemplation of verses 41–45 with the incisive psychological diagnosis of hypocrisy that follows in verses 47–50.

The transition carries profound pedagogical weight: how can a human being contemplate the majestic cosmic order—where skies, birds, clouds, and all living creatures submit seamlessly to divine decree—and then turn around and practice duplicity, refusing to submit their personal disputes to the judgment of God and His Messenger? Verse 46 reminds humanity that the same God who orders the heavens and orchestrates the rainfall has revealed clear moral statutes to govern human society with absolute justice. To marvel at the physical universe while resisting divine moral authority is an intolerable contradiction that exposes the deep pathology of the human ego, calling the soul back to consistent integrity, wholehearted devotion, and sincere obedience before its Lord.

---
## Sūrah an-Nūr 24:47

> And the hypocrites say, “We believe in Allah and the Messenger, and we obey.” Then a group of them turns away soon after that. These are not ˹true˺ believers.

**Expanded Commentary**

**Verbal Profession and Moral Performance: wa-yaqūlūna āmannā**

The verse initiates an incisive diagnostic exposure of hypocrisy (*al-nifāq*) within the Madinan polity: *wa-yaqūlūna āmannā bi-Allāhi wa-bi-al-rasūli wa-aṭaʿnā*. The verb *yaqūlūn* (they say) immediately establishes a critical discrepancy between verbal articulation and genuine internal conviction.

The hypocrites were masters of theatrical piety and rhetorical eloquence. In public gatherings, communal councils, and congregational spaces, they enthusiastically proclaimed their faith in God and His Messenger, adding the solemn commitment: *wa-aṭaʿnā* (and we obey!). They utilized Islamic terminology, mirrored external religious postures, and sought social capital by aligning themselves verbally with the ascendant Muslim community. In tribal and communal societies, public speech functions as a social currency. The hypocrites sought to reap all the protective benefits, social status, political influence, and economic advantages of communal belonging without paying the necessary price of internal submission, spiritual discipline, or ethical sacrifice.

**The Test of Practical Obedience: wa-aṭaʿnā**

The word *aṭaʿnā* (we obey) represents the ultimate battlefield of religious authenticity. It is easy, emotionally gratifying, and socially advantageous to proclaim loyalty to an ideal when that ideal demands no personal cost or material sacrifice.

True obedience (*al-ṭāʿah*), however, requires the active surrender of personal desires, financial interests, and tribal loyalties to the sovereign authority of divine law. The moment the commands of God and His Messenger align with self-interest, obedience is effortless; but the moment divine law contradicts an individual's personal ambition, economic profit, or familial pride, the authentic character of their commitment is laid bare. The claim of obedience made by the hypocrites was entirely conditional, lasting only as long as Islamic governance flattered their egos and preserved their material interests without demanding concrete sacrifice, humility, or legal concessions, exposing their utter lack of genuine devotion.

**The Rupture of Defection: thumma yatawallā farīqun minhum**

The text exposes the rapid unraveling of their verbal contract: *thumma yatawallā farīqun minhum min baʿdi dhālik*, then a group of them turns away soon after that. The particle *thumma* denotes a temporal and logical progression that highlights an astonishing moral inconsistency between their speech and actions.

No sooner had the pious words left their lips than a faction among them turned on their heels, retreating from concrete obligations. The verb *yatawallā* signifies deliberate, disdainful turning away, physically and emotionally distancing oneself from prophetic leadership. This defection occurred not after years of gradual ideological erosion, but *min baʿdi dhālik*—immediately following their solemn verbal oaths of obedience! When actual testing arrived—whether in the form of a legal judgment that disfavored them, a mandatory military deployment, or a financial contribution—their superficial commitment evaporated, revealing the hollow core beneath their pious declarations and showing the world their true character, bankrupt of all moral courage.

**The Epistemological Hollow of Hypocrisy**

Islamic psychology views hypocrisy as a profound state of cognitive dissonance, spiritual fragmentation, and emotional exhaustion. The hypocrite lives in perpetual terror of exposure, constantly maintaining a double identity: an external mask of conformity designed for public consumption, and an internal reality of cynicism, resentment, and skepticism.

This internal duality fractures the human soul, destroying peace of mind. The hypocrite cannot experience the tranquil unity of the sincere believer whose heart, tongue, and limbs operate in harmonious alignment. Instead, they expend immense cognitive energy calculating risks, managing appearances, and hedging bets between competing social forces. This chronic duplicity calcifies the conscience, making the hypocrite increasingly immune to spiritual admonition, until they become completely incapable of discerning truth from falsehood, trapped in a self-constructed prison of deceit, suspicion, and paralyzing anxiety that darkens every aspect of their existence.

**Divine Stripping of the Faithful Identity: wa-mā ulāʾika bi-al-muʾminīn**

The verse concludes with an uncompromising, categorical verdict delivered by the Sovereign Lord: *wa-mā ulāʾika bi-al-muʾminīn*, and these are not true believers. The syntax employs the strongest form of Arabic negation (*mā* followed by the preposition *bi* attached to the predicate), completely dismantling their religious pretenses.

God does not evaluate human beings based on eloquent slogans, elaborate titles, or performative rituals. Faith (*īmān*) is not a subjective emotional sentiment, nor a superficial tribal badge that confers automatic immunity. In the divine ledger, faith is an indissoluble synthesis of internal conviction (*taṣdīq*), verbal affirmation (*iqrār*), and righteous bodily deeds (*ʿamal*). By turning away from the practical commands of the Messenger ﷺ, the hypocrites demonstrated that their verbal claims were fraudulent. The divine verdict strips them of the honorable title of believer, warning the community against being deceived by loud professions of piety that lack the substance of obedient practice, establishing that true belief requires faithful action and sincere submission.

**The Sociological Danger of Performative Religion**

This diagnostic exposure carries enduring relevance for every generation of religious communities. Performative religion—where external displays of orthodoxy mask deep ethical corruption, social cruelty, and selective obedience—is far more destructive to the spiritual health of a society than open, honest disbelief.

An open opponent is identifiable and can be engaged directly. A hypocrite operates from within the community, corroding trust, poisoning social cohesion, and sowing cynical doubt among vulnerable members. When leaders or prominent community figures proclaim piety with their mouths while consistently violating basic standards of justice and integrity in private or commercial dealings, they inflict catastrophic damage upon the credibility of faith itself, driving seekers away into skepticism. Verse 47 serves as a permanent mirror, demanding that every believer rigorously audit their own soul to ensure that their daily actions validate, rather than contradict, the faith they profess before the world, purifying both public conduct and secret intention to attain divine acceptance, enduring honor, and eternal salvation.

---
## Sūrah an-Nūr 24:48

> And as soon as they are called to Allah and His Messenger so he may judge between them, a group of them turns away.

**Expanded Commentary**

**The Judicial Summons: idhā duʿū ilā Allāh**

The verse isolates the precise moment of testing where the hypocrisy of the disaffected was laid bare: *wa-idhā duʿū ilā Allāhi wa-rasūlihī li-yaḥkuma baynahum idhā farīqun minhum muʿriḍūn*. The phrase *duʿū ilā Allāh* (when they are called to Allah and His Messenger) refers to a formal summons to judicial arbitration in civil, commercial, or property disputes within the Madinan community.

In the nascent state of Madinah, the Prophet Muḥammad ﷺ was not merely a spiritual preacher; he was the head of state and supreme constitutional magistrate. Submitting a legal dispute to him meant subjecting personal claims to divine revelation and unyielding prophetic integrity. For the sincere believer, such a summons was an occasion of relief, peace, and gratitude, knowing that their rights would be adjudicated with immaculate fairness and profound wisdom. For the hypocrite, however, the summons was an existential threat, because divine justice recognizes no social bribery, tribal favoritism, or elite entitlement, holding all parties strictly accountable before the law.

**The Singular Authority of the Prophet: li-yaḥkuma baynahum**

The syntactic phrasing of the purpose clause contains an exquisite theological nuance: *li-yaḥkuma baynahum* (so he may judge between them). The singular masculine pronoun *yaḥkum* (he judges) refers grammatically to the Messenger ﷺ, yet the summons was explicitly to *Allah and His Messenger*.

Classical commentators, including al-Rāzī, al-Ṭabarī, and Ibn Kathīr, observed that this syntactic fusion affirms the absolute inseparability of divine law and prophetic judgment. The Messenger ﷺ does not judge according to personal whim, cultural convention, or subjective bias; his rulings are direct applications and embodiments of divine revelation. To accept the judgment of the Prophet is to accept the judgment of God; conversely, to reject or evade prophetic adjudication is an explicit rejection of divine sovereignty itself.
